TO anon`, a new `SECURITY DEFINER` helper `ticket_belongs_to_caller()` (same pattern as `has_duty_access`/`has_role`, bypasses RLS internally while enforcing the identical boundary) replacing the subquery, and a client-side fix in `TicketForm.tsx`: the ticket id is now generated with `crypto.randomUUID()` and both inserts drop `.select()` entirely, so nothing ever needs to be read back under RLS. The email field is now `required` in the UI, matching the guest insert policy's own `guest_email` requirement, and the form's copy was reworded from internal "ticket" language ("Submit Ticket", "Ticket Created!") to plain "Send message" / "Message sent" for a public-facing surface. Verified live end to end as a real signed-out visitor: submitted the form on `/contact`, confirmed both rows landed in `support_tickets` and `ticket_messages` by direct query, then confirmed as a separate negative test that an anonymous caller still cannot post into a real authenticated user's ticket (the same RLS boundary that protected real accounts before this change still holds). Test rows cleaned up after.

New shared `src/components/shared/SectionHeading.tsx`, a small ember accent bar beside the heading text, used for every section `<h2>` across all three pages (9 headings total: About's "The problem"/"How we got here"/"What it does", Help's per-category headings plus "Still stuck"/"Send us a message", Contact's "Email"/"Before you write"/"Specific requests"/"Postal"/"What we do not have"). Preceded by v3.47.0, three more admin gaps closed: an activity log, real email delivery visibility, and a plan editor — and a genuine bug found and fixed live while building the second one.

ACTIVITY LOG, EMAIL DELIVERY VISIBILITY, AND A PLAN EDITOR. Continuation of the same "what am I missing as admin" list from v3.46.0; the founder asked for the next three.

`get_admin_activity_log()` is the first reader `security_audit_logs` has ever had — nearly every `admin_*` RPC since early in this project has written to it and nothing ever read it back. New System → Activity pane (`ActivityPane`, `SystemPanes.tsx`) shows who did what and when, in plain English (`ACTIVITY_LABELS` maps raw action names like `admin_suspend_account` to "Suspended an account"), with a compact one-line summary of the jsonb `details` column and a high-severity badge.

New System → Admins-adjacent Money → "Edit plans" card (`PlanEditor`, `ControlPanes.tsx`, backed by `get_admin_plans`/`admin_update_plan`) lets an admin change a plan's name, credits, proposals/assessments/searches limits, and whether it's visible to new signups — all without touching the database directly, for the first time. `price_cents` and the Stripe price/product ids are deliberately left read-only: Stripe prices are immutable once created, and this project has never once created or mutated a Stripe Price/Product object anywhere (confirmed by grepping every edge function), so editing the displayed price here without a matching Stripe-side change would silently desync what the app shows from what people are actually charged, a worse bug than not having the editor. Verified live on `seeker_week` (Week pass), the one seeker plan with zero real subscribers: changed credits 30 → 31, confirmed the change, then restored it to exactly 30.

REAL BUG FOUND WHILE BUILDING EMAIL DELIVERY VISIBILITY. The third piece, `get_admin_email_log()` plus a new "Did these emails actually go out?" card in the Email pane (`EmailLogSection`, reading the same `email_logs` table `admin-broadcast` already wrote to), needed the v3.43.0/v3.44.0 automatic emails to actually write to `email_logs` for the first time — they never had. Wiring that up (`stripe-webhook`'s `sendReceiptEmail`, `resume-hub`'s `notifyCandidate`/`notifyOrgMembers`, and a brand new service-role client added to `auth-send-email`, which previously had no database access at all) surfaced a real, previously invisible bug: `auth-send-email`'s signup confirmation email was silently failing to log itself on **every single signup**, always. Root cause, found by temporarily mirroring the real Postgres error into `security_audit_logs` (already proven to work, already readable via the new Activity pane) since this function's own console output isn't visible to us: a `23503` foreign key violation, `email_logs.user_id` referencing `auth.users(id)`, because the Supabase Auth "Send Email" hook fires *before* a brand-new signup's `auth.users` row is visible to the hook's own database connection — the row genuinely doesn't exist yet from that connection's point of view at the moment the hook runs. `recovery` and `email_change` emails were never affected, since those always target an already-existing user. Fixed by dropping `user_id` from `auth-send-email`'s `email_logs` writes entirely; `recipient_email` alone is enough to identify who an email was for. Verified live end to end three times: a real signup confirmation now logs `status: 'sent'` correctly; a real proposal-notification send (an already-existing account, no FK risk) also logs correctly, and happened to genuinely fail in the same test (Resend rejects `@example.com` as a test domain), which the log correctly captured with Resend's real error message — an unplanned but perfect proof that failures, not just successes, now show up.

Details in docs/map/platform.md and docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.46.0, a confirmation step on the kill switches and a new System → Admins pane to see and manage who has admin access.

TWO REAL GAPS CLOSED AFTER THE FOUNDER ASKED "WHAT AM I MISSING AS ADMIN?". Asked directly, after the v3.45.0 email reference shipped, what a normal admin panel would have that this one didn't. Answered with a short list; the founder picked the two worth building now.

KILL SWITCHES NO LONGER FIRE ON A MISCLICK. `FlagsPane` (`src/components/admin/sections/system/ControlPanes.tsx`) called `set.mutate` the instant any switch was toggled, including "Whole platform," which puts every signed in surface into maintenance for every user immediately. One misclick, no undo but flipping it back. Turning a switch OFF now asks `window.confirm` first, naming the switch and stating it takes effect immediately; turning one back ON is unguarded, since that's the safe, restorative direction and gating it would only slow down recovering from a real incident. Verified live: stubbed `window.confirm` to return false and clicked "Candidate search" off, confirmed the switch stayed on and no mutation fired — did not test the positive path live, since that would mean actually flipping a real production flag, and the underlying `set.mutate` call is unchanged, already-proven code.

ADMIN ACCESS WAS INVISIBLE AND UNMANAGEABLE FROM THE PANEL. There was no way to see who had the admin role, or to grant or remove it, without an engineer running SQL by hand. New RPCs `get_admin_admins()` (lists every account with the admin role: email, when granted, last sign in) and `admin_set_admin_role(p_user_id, p_grant, p_reason)` (grants or revokes, admin-only, requires a reason, audits at high severity), both `supabase/migrations/20260803200000_admin_role_management.sql`. `admin_set_admin_role` refuses to target your own account, the same self-lockout protection every other admin moderation action already has (suspend, restrict, erase). New System → Admins pane (`AdminsPane` in `ControlPanes.tsx`): a list of current admins with a "You" badge on your own row and no remove button on it, plus a search-and-grant form reusing the same account search the Credits pane already has. Found and fixed one bug while building it: the same `Badge`-inside-`<p>` invalid DOM nesting the v3.41.0 walkthrough found and fixed once already in `EmployersSection.tsx`, reintroduced fresh in this new pane's "You" badge — same fix, wrap in a `<div>` instead. Verified live with two throwaway accounts (one made admin to sign in as, one granted and then revoked admin access as the test subject): the self-protection correctly hid the remove button and showed "You" only on the signed-in account's own row, granting a real account made it appear in the list immediately, revoking it removed it immediately, and the real founder admin account was never touched.

Preceded by v3.45.0, a plain-English reference list of every automatic system email, added to the admin System → Email pane.

ADMIN EMAIL PANE NOW EXPLAINS ITSELF. Asked directly, after the v3.44.0 notification emails shipped, to make the admin panel explain things in plain English rather than requiring the founder to ask what something means or read code — and specifically to show, in the Email pane, what AYN actually sends on its own. `SystemPanes.tsx`'s `EmailPane` previously showed only the broadcast composer (write a message, pick an audience, send), with no visibility into the nine automatic emails covered under v3.44.0 and v3.43.0. Added a collapsible `SystemEmailsReference` card above the composer, static content (fixed templates, nothing to fetch), one entry per email: subject line, who it goes to, when it fires, and a one-line plain-English description of what it says, deliberately avoiding words like "webhook," "hook," or "RPC." The composer below it is now labeled "Write your own message" so the two are visually distinct rather than looking like one tool. Verified live: signed into the real admin panel with a throwaway admin account, opened System → Email, expanded the new card, confirmed all nine entries render correctly with no console errors, collapsed and re-expanded it, then cleaned up the test account and restored the PIN hash exactly as done throughout this session.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.44.0, four new notification emails: credit/subscription receipts, proposal received, assessment received, and employer notified of a candidate response.

FOUR NEW NOTIFICATION EMAILS, WHERE THERE WERE PREVIOUSLY ZERO. Asked directly: what happens by email when someone buys credits, or a job seeker gets a proposal or assessment, or an employer's candidate responds? Answer, confirmed by reading every relevant handler: nothing did. `stripe-webhook`'s `invoice.paid` only called `credit_grant`, `employer_reveal_request`/`employer_assessment_send` only wrote rows, `reveal_decide`/`assessment_submit` only updated rows — no email, no notifications table, no bell, nothing besides a badge count the person has to go look at. Built all four:

New shared `supabase/functions/_shared/emailTemplate.ts` — one copy of the branded card shell (`wrapEmail`, `ctaButton`, `heading`, `para`, `receiptRow`, `escapeHtml`, `sendBrandedEmail`) matching the `.lp` design tokens, factored out of the v3.43.0 auth-send-email rewrite so every future transactional email starts from the same look instead of copy-pasting HTML again. `auth-send-email` itself was left as is, not refactored onto this, since it was already correct and working and didn't need touching.

`stripe-webhook`'s `invoice.paid` handler now emails a payment receipt (plan, amount charged, credits added) right after the existing `credit_grant` call, best effort only, wrapped so a Resend failure can never undo a real charge that already succeeded.

`resume-hub` gained two small helpers, `notifyCandidate` and `notifyOrgMembers` (the latter emails every org member, not just whoever is signed in — the schema has always supported multi-seat orgs even though production is solo employers today), both swallowing their own errors so a failed send can never fail the action that triggered it. Wired into `employer_reveal_request` (candidate emailed a new proposal), `employer_assessment_send` (candidate emailed a new assessment), `reveal_decide` (employer emailed on accept or decline), and `finaliseAssessment` (employer emailed when a candidate submits). Every notify call is `await`ed rather than fire-and-forget, since a Deno edge function isolate can be torn down right after the response is sent and an un-awaited background call can silently never complete. Employer-facing emails deliberately carry no candidate name, score, or answer content, matching the product's own existing anonymity rules (v3.6.0, v3.13.0) — just a heads up and a link back into the app.

Verified live: two real throwaway accounts (a seeker and an employer with an approved org), a real proposal sent through `employer_reveal_request` (candidate notify path, `notifyCandidate`) and a real accept through `reveal_decide` (employer notify path, `notifyOrgMembers`) — both calls succeeded, and the proposal call's execution time (5s, versus the usual 1 to 2s for this action) is consistent with the added Resend round trip actually running. The assessment-side wiring reuses the identical two helpers already proven live by these two calls, so it was verified by code read-back rather than a second full live setup. The Stripe receipt could not be triggered live without either the webhook signing secret (not available here) or a real Stripe test purchase (out of scope to attempt), so that one rests on code review and the same proven `sendBrandedEmail` plumbing, not a live send.

Details in docs/map/platform.md and docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.43.0, rebranding every sign up / sign in email to match the current "Charcoal & Ember" product design.

AUTH EMAILS REBRANDED, AND FINALLY TRACKED IN THIS REPO. Asked directly to fix the branding on the emails users get for sign up and sign in. The real send path is `auth-send-email`, the Supabase Auth "Send Email" hook target that GoTrue calls for every `signup`, `recovery`, `magiclink` and `email_change` event (wired via the Supabase Dashboard's Auth Hooks config, not anything in this repo) — and it had **no local source anywhere in this repository**, existing only live on the server, version 417, since before this repo's history can show. Two other auth-email-shaped things exist and are both dead ends, confirmed not to touch: `custom-auth-emails` requires an admin JWT, which structurally can't be a hook target GoTrue calls itself, and has zero callers in `src/`; the `auth_email_templates` table it reads from is seeded with old purple-gradient copy nothing live ever shows. Pulled `auth-send-email`'s real source via the Supabase API, found it still used the pre-rebrand dark theme (`#0f0f0f` page, `#1a1a1a` card, white-on-dark CTA button, plain "AYN. All rights reserved." footer) that predates the landing page's current look entirely. Rewrote the template to match the `.lp` design tokens used everywhere else (`src/index.css`) — warm paper page, white card, `#0b0b0c` ink, `#e85d3a` ember accent on the CTA button and as a small underline below the wordmark, `AYN AI` in the footer matching `siteLinks.ts`'s `COPYRIGHT_LINE` — using `admin-broadcast`'s `wrap()` as the closest existing structural reference in the repo, since that one already tracked the current palette (just without any ember accent of its own). No functional logic touched: webhook signature verification, URL building, and the Resend call are byte-for-byte the same as before. The file is now committed to `supabase/functions/auth-send-email/index.ts`, closing the same kind of "lives only on the server" gap the v3.27.0 migration-history fix closed for six admin functions. Verified live: redeployed, read the deployed source back and confirmed it matched exactly, then triggered a real signup through a throwaway account — `confirmation_sent_at` came back set on a clean 200, which only happens if the (blocking) Send Email hook itself returned success, meaning the new template rendered and sent through Resend without error.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.42.0, locking the credit functions against a real concurrent-overspend race flagged during the v3.41.0 admin panel walkthrough.

CREDIT FUNCTIONS LOCKED AGAINST CONCURRENT OVERSPEND. `billing_ensure`, `credit_spend` and `credit_grant` each did a plain read-balance-then-write with no row locking, and all three are independently callable (`resume-hub` calls all three directly; `stripe-webhook` calls `credit_grant` directly; `credit_spend` also calls `billing_ensure` internally). Two near-simultaneous calls for the same user — a double click, two tabs, a retry after a slow-but-successful response — could both read the same starting balance before either committed, both pass the sufficiency check, and both write a deduction: a real overspend, not just a cosmetic ledger mismatch. Flagged rather than fixed during the v3.41.0 walkthrough since it needed a locking-strategy decision, not a one-line patch. Fixed with a per-user `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(user_id::text))` taken as the first statement in all three functions: it serializes concurrent calls for the same user only (different users never block each other), needs no schema change, auto-releases at transaction end even on error, and works for a brand-new user with no `subscriptions` row yet (unlike locking an existing row, which `billing_ensure` is what creates in the first place). Advisory locks are reentrant per transaction, so `credit_spend` taking the lock and then calling `billing_ensure` (which takes the same lock again) is a safe, instant no-op re-acquire, not a deadlock. Verified with two synthetic concurrent `credit_spend` calls sharing one balance: no overspend, final balance matched the ledger sum exactly, and the second call was correctly refused for insufficient funds once the first had already spent it down.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.41.0, a full live walkthrough of every pane in the admin panel, checking for bugs and broken features after v3.40.0's grant hardening.

ADMIN PANEL WALKTHROUGH — FOUR REAL BUGS, EVERYTHING ELSE CONFIRMED WORKING. Asked directly to check the whole admin panel for bugs and broken features after the v3.40.0 grant changes. Walked all six top-level sections and all 11 System sub-panes live against production with a throwaway admin account. Found and fixed four real bugs, none related to the v3.40.0 grants themselves (those were confirmed unaffected: every hardened `admin_*`/`get_admin_*` function still works correctly end to end, exercised through `AccountDetailDialog`'s suspend/restore/restrict/limit-override/erase/purge and through `admin_adjust_credits`).

BUG — LOCAL ADMIN PANEL TESTING WAS STRUCTURALLY IMPOSSIBLE. `admin-auth-pin` is the only edge function gated by `_shared/originGuard.ts`'s real origin allowlist (`resume-hub` uses a wide-open `*`); that allowlist listed `localhost:5173` and `:8080` but this project's actual dev server runs on `:3000` (`vite.config.ts`), so every local PIN submission CORS-failed before reaching the function and surfaced as a misleading "Incorrect PIN" even with a correct PIN. Confirmed by a direct `curl` bypassing browser CORS returning `success:true` for the exact PIN the browser had just rejected, and confirmed the failed browser attempt never reached the server at all (`admin_pin_attempts` unchanged). Fixed by adding `http://localhost:3000` to `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` and redeploying `admin-auth-pin`.

BUG — `<Badge>` NESTED INSIDE `<p>` IN EMPLOYERS SECTION. `EmployersSection.tsx`'s approved/declined/suspended list wrapped a `Badge` (which renders a root `<div>`) inside a `<p className="font-semibold flex items-center gap-2">`, invalid HTML that React's `validateDOMNesting` flags and the browser silently auto-closes early. Changed the wrapping element to a `<div>`; confirmed via `tsc` and direct DOM inspection (`badge.parentElement.tagName === "DIV"`) that no other admin section has the same pattern.

BUG — CREDITS PANE SNAPSHOT ALWAYS EMPTY. `admin_user_snapshot` (backs the System → Credits pane's read-only Snapshot panel) referenced a column that has never existed, `talent_pool_consent.tp.is_active`; every other place in the codebase correctly uses `opted_in`. The function raised `42703` on every call, which `CreditsPane` silently swallowed (branching only on `isLoading`, never `isError`), so the panel just showed permanent empty defaults (Email —, Credits 0, Plan none) no matter which account was selected, with no visible error. Fixed the column reference; verified live the panel now shows real data (credits, plan, recent credit history) for a real account.

BUG — TWO MISSING GRANTS, SAME SHAPE AS THE v3.27.0 TERMS-CONSENT BUG. `get_admin_rate_limit_stats` (loads the System → Rate limits pane) and `admin_unblock_user` (its Unblock button) were both missing the `authenticated` GRANT every sibling `get_admin_*`/`admin_*` function has, so the Rate limits pane 403'd on load for every real admin. `get_admin_ai_cost_stats` was also found ungranted but confirmed orphaned (zero callers in `src/`; the real AI cost pane uses `get_admin_ai_usage`, which was already correctly granted) — left alone, matching the existing pattern of harmless orphaned admin RPCs (`delete-account`, v3.35.0). Fixed the two real gaps with the missing GRANTs.

BUG — HEADLINE "JOB SEEKERS" STAT UNDERCOUNTED BY MORE THAN HALF. `get_admin_overview` (Overview page) and `get_admin_accounts` (System → Accounts pane) both counted job seekers as `SELECT count(*) FROM profiles WHERE coalesce(role,'job_seeker')='job_seeker'` — a query that only iterates rows already present in `profiles`. 31 of 58 real accounts have no `profiles` row at all (never completed onboarding) and were silently excluded: Overview showed 27 job seekers where the real number is 57. `get_admin_email_audience` already computed this correctly by LEFT JOINing from `auth.users`; a first fix attempt copied that LEFT JOIN pattern but still undercounted the non-seeker side by one, because the one real employer account's `profiles.role` is stale at `'job_seeker'` (it signed up before the v3.36.0 role-on-signup fix landed, despite having a real, approved `employer_accounts` row) — `profiles.role` cannot be trusted as the seeker/employer split. Final fix: "job seeker" now means "has no row in `employer_accounts`", independent of `profiles.role`, matching how `get_admin_email_audience` already treats `is_employer`. Verified live: both stats now read 57 seekers / 1 employer / 58 total, consistent with every other pane.

NOT A BUG, FOUND ALONG THE WAY. `admin_adjust_credits`'s ledger `balance_after` can look wrong on an account's very first spend: deducting credits from an account that has never had `billing_ensure` run for it (no `subscriptions` row yet) triggers a same-transaction `period_grant` of the plan's free credits before the deduction, so the deduct row's `balance_after` reflects `old_balance + period_grant - deduct`, not a simple subtraction. Confirmed this is a real design quirk of `billing_ensure`/`credit_spend` (both plain read-then-write with no row locking, `supabase/migrations/20260731122450_*.sql` and `20260731235555_*.sql`), not a concurrency bug and not something this walkthrough fixed — flagged here since it could look alarming in the ledger without this context, and separately because `credit_spend`'s unlocked balance check is a genuine (if narrow) real-overspend race under true concurrent spends, worth the founder's own look.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.40.0, a full bug and security audit: Supabase's own advisor plus a manual pass over RLS, grants, edge functions and the frontend.

SECURITY — 14 SECURITY DEFINER FUNCTIONS EXPOSED TO ANON, NONE ACTUALLY EXPLOITABLE. Supabase's security advisor flagged `admin_clear_limit_override`, `admin_erase_account`, `admin_purge_account`, `admin_restore_account`, `admin_set_limit_override`, `admin_set_restriction`, `admin_suspend_account`, `get_admin_account_detail`, `get_admin_account_governance` (via an inherited PUBLIC grant) and `self_delete_account`, `self_export_account`, `self_list_sessions`, `self_pause_account`, `self_revoke_session` (explicit anon grant) as callable by the fully unauthenticated anon role. Checked every one of the 14: each enforces its own check as its first statement, `has_role(auth.uid(),'admin')` for the admin ones or `auth.uid() IS NULL` for the self ones, and `has_role(NULL,'admin')` was confirmed to return false (SQL NULL never equals anything in the `WHERE user_id = _user_id` check), so none of this was live exploitable. Tightened anyway as defense in depth: REVOKE from PUBLIC and anon, GRANT to authenticated and service_role only, matching the restrictive pattern already used for assessment_rubrics/assessment_results. `get_feature_flags` was deliberately left anon-accessible; the signed-out landing page needs it pre-login and it holds nothing sensitive. Verified live: a real authenticated call to self_list_sessions still returns real data, an anon call now gets a 42501 permission-denied at the grant level instead of reaching the function body at all.

SECURITY — REAL HTML INJECTION IN ADMIN-BROADCAST. `admin-broadcast`'s `wrap(subject, message)` interpolated both admin-authored fields straight into the outgoing HTML email with no escaping; an admin typing `<a href="...">` into either field would ship as real, clickable markup to every recipient in the audience. `send-contact-email` and `send-ticket-reply` (also reviewed, no source in this repo, pulled live) already escape correctly, so this was an inconsistency, not a systemic gap. Fixed with the same `escapeHtml` those two already use, applied to both fields inside `wrap()` only; the plain-text envelope Subject header and `email_logs` metadata are intentionally left unescaped since neither renders HTML. Verified: an isolated run of the exact deployed logic confirmed a benign message renders unchanged and `<script>alert(1)</script>` now renders as literal escaped text.

SECURITY — CONFIRMED CLEAN, NO ACTION NEEDED. Every table in `public` has RLS enabled, zero exceptions. The three "RLS enabled, no policies" findings (`assessment_results`, `assessment_rubrics`, `job_cache`) are the v3.13.0 service-role-only design working as intended, not gaps: no policy plus RLS on means deny-by-default to every role except service_role, which bypasses RLS entirely. Every `USING (true)` policy found is a SELECT on the World Intelligence reference tables (out of scope, no PII, read-only broadcast data); every write policy on those same tables is service_role only. Stripe webhook signature verification (`stripe.webhooks.constructEventAsync` against `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`) is correctly implemented; nobody can forge a billing event. A real `.env` was found committed to git history (2025-08-28, removed 2026-04-07, still fully recoverable since history was never rewritten) but its only contents both times were `VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` and `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`, the same values already intentionally hardcoded in committed `src/config.ts` today — not a real secret leak.

SECURITY — NOTED, NOT ACTED ON. `resume-hub`'s own CORS is `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` while `admin-auth-pin`/`admin-broadcast` restrict to the real origin allowlist; lower risk since auth here is a bearer JWT per request, not a cookie, but worth tightening for consistency someday. `auth_leaked_password_protection` (HaveIBeenPwned check on new passwords) is off; this is a Supabase dashboard toggle with no migration or API path available here, needs the founder's own hand. The `vector` extension lives in the public schema (Supabase advisor default complaint); moving it means recreating every dependent column, not worth the disruption for the actual risk. `has_duty_access` and the `'duty'` role value still exist in the schema and in `useAuth.ts`, but `useAuth.ts` has zero importers anywhere in `src/` and nothing calls `has_duty_access` as an RPC, matching what v3.26.0 already said about dropping duty from every real gate: confirmed fully inert, not a live bypass, left alone rather than touching the enum.

BUG — RESUME UPLOAD SILENTLY DISCARDED ITS OWN EXTRACTION. `ProfileTab.tsx`'s `handleResumeParsed` merged the freshly parsed resume's skills/experience/education into local `career` state, then called the full `load()` to refresh the resumes list — but `load()` also unconditionally re-fetched `career` from `user_profile_canonical` and overwrote it, before the 900ms autosave debounce ever got a chance to persist the merge. Net effect: uploading a new resume saved the resume file itself but never actually updated the matching profile it is supposed to populate. Same shape of bug as v3.36.0's CompanyProfile.tsx finding: a full reload stomping local state that has not been saved yet. Fixed by extracting a `loadResumes()` that only touches resume-list state, called instead of the full `load()` after an upload. Verified live: seeded an existing profile with distinct skills, uploaded a resume with entirely different ones through a real simulated file drop, confirmed via direct query that `user_profile_canonical.skills` ended up with the new resume's skills, not the old ones.

BUG — CONNECTING THE EXTENSION LOST THE WHOLE FLOW IF YOU WERE SIGNED OUT. `ExtensionApprove.tsx`'s "Sign in to continue" wrote a `post_login_redirect` to sessionStorage and navigated to `/?signin=1`; neither was ever read anywhere in the codebase, so signing in dropped the user on the plain landing page with the approval code, and the whole in-progress link, gone. Fixed by rendering the same `AuthModal` used elsewhere directly on this page instead of navigating away at all, with an `onAuthStateChange` listener flipping the page's own state to ready the moment a session appears; the code param never leaves this page's URL. Verified live end to end with a real throwaway account: opened the modal in place, signed in, state correctly showed the right email with the code intact, and clicking Approve completed the real link_approve call ("Browser connected").

BUG — ASSESSMENT AUTO-SUBMIT COULD FIRE TWICE. `AssessmentsTab.tsx`'s countdown re-ran `submit(true)` every second once the deadline passed, guarded only on `active` (which does not clear until the request resolves), not on `busy`. On a slow connection the next tick could fire a second concurrent `assessment_submit` call before the first cleared `active`. Backend already guards against real harm (`finaliseAssessment` returns immediately if `submitted_at` is already set, and the results write is an upsert keyed on `assessment_id`), so the actual damage was bounded to an occasional wasted AI grading call, never duplicate rows or a wrong score. Fixed anyway: `submit` now also checks `busy` before running, with `busy` added to its own dependency array so the check is never stale.

Details in docs/map/platform.md and docs/map/resume-hub.md.

Preceded by v3.39.0, sign out did nothing on either job seeker or employer surfaces.

SIGN OUT SILENTLY DID NOTHING. Reported directly: a job seeker clicking Sign out in Resume Hub saw no change at all. `ResumeHub.tsx`'s dropdown item called a bare `supabase.auth.signOut()` with no `await` and no navigation after it, and neither `ResumeHub.tsx` nor `EmployerHub.tsx` (found in passing, same pattern at two separate buttons: desktop sidebar and mobile bottom nav) has an `onAuthStateChange` listener of its own, unlike `Index.tsx`'s `AuthedShell`. The Supabase session genuinely cleared underneath, but since both pages are top-level routes rendered outside `AuthedShell`, nothing was listening for `SIGNED_OUT` to react to it, so the fully-gated page just sat there looking untouched, JWT gone, every subsequent action about to fail. Fixed the same way `EmployerPending.tsx` and `PrivacySettings.tsx`'s delete-account flow already did it correctly: `await supabase.auth.signOut()` then `navigate("/")`, added as a shared `handleSignOut` in each file. Admin's own sign out (`AdminApp.tsx`, `AdminPanel.tsx`) uses a separate, non-persisted `adminSupabase` client with its own step-based flow and was left alone, not what was reported and a different risk profile. Verified live end to end for both surfaces with real throwaway accounts: signed in, clicked Sign out, confirmed the redirect to the landing page and an empty `localStorage` (no `sb-*` keys left behind).

Details in docs/map/resume-hub.md and docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.37.0, the extension could silently score and tailor against the wrong job on any master/detail job list, a tailor/cover-letter quality check, and candidate vector recall moved into Postgres to actually use the index built for it.

LINKEDIN (AND ANY SIMILAR LIST) COULD SCORE THE WRONG JOB. Reported directly: clicking a job in a list view sometimes scored and tailored against a different job than the one just opened. Root cause was in the extension, not the backend: `background.js`'s JD resolver ladder documents itself as short-circuiting at the first step that clears a quality threshold, but the code actually collected every step (current page, opener tab, registry fuzzy match) unconditionally and picked whichever scored highest, ignoring ladder order. Compounding it, `jdKey(url)` dropped the query string entirely, so every job on LinkedIn's `/jobs/search` or `/jobs/collections/*` (same pathname, only `currentJobId` differs) collapsed onto one `JD_REGISTRY` slot; an earlier, higher-scoring job could sit there for the full 45 minute TTL and silently outrank a correct live read of whatever is actually open now. Fixed both: `jdKey` keeps the same param allowlist `normalizeUrlForHash` already uses server side, and the ladder now genuinely stops at the first step (current page) that clears the threshold instead of comparing it against a stale registry entry. `content.js` also gained a staleness retry: on a route change, text identical to what was last reported for a different URL means the detail pane has not caught up yet, so it retries with backoff instead of reporting it. Found in passing: the LinkedIn selectors only matched the signed-in unified pane; the signed-out public job search view uses different classes entirely and returned zero extraction, now fixed with both selector sets listed together. Verified with isolated reproductions of the exact collision (two LinkedIn URLs differing only by `currentJobId`, old `jdKey` collapsed them, new one does not; old ladder picked a stale higher-quality registry entry over a good live read, new ladder picks the live read) and the signed-out selector fix confirmed directly against the live DOM. Not verified end to end signed in, since this tooling cannot load an unpacked MV3 extension or hold a real LinkedIn session. Extension version bumped to 3.4.0 and rebuilt (sideload only, no auto update, so this does not reach anyone until they reinstall).

TAILOR AND COVER LETTER, RECHECKED FOR QUALITY. Also asked to confirm rather than only reconfirm credits: ran a real tailor and cover letter call through a throwaway account with a resume carrying deliberately specific, checkable facts (exact numbers, company names, dates) against a JD with both overlapping skills and a skill genuinely absent from the resume (Kubernetes). Every fact survived unchanged (company names, dates, "4 hours to 40 minutes", "35% to 78%", "40,000 daily active users"), nothing was invented (Kubernetes never appeared), and the cover letter followed its own style rules (no clichés, no em or en dashes, under 280 words). Credits charged correctly and in sequence (6 to 4 to 3). No bug found; this is a confirmation, not a fix.

THE CANDIDATE POOL IS NOT A GRAPH. Asked directly: it is a plain Postgres table (`candidate_index`), one row per opted-in candidate, holding a 768-dim pgvector column (`openai/text-embedding-3-small`, deterministic hash fallback if the AI gateway fails) plus extracted/inferred skills in a sibling table. No knowledge graph, no graph-memory library, nothing graph-shaped anywhere in this path. The match pipeline (`employer_match` in resume-hub) is the same three stages described under "Employer mode" below: a deterministic must-have prefilter using extracted-only skills, then vector similarity recall, then a grounded LLM rerank on anonymized refs.

VECTOR RECALL MOVED INTO POSTGRES (same v3.37.0 session, asked as a follow up). `candidate_index` has always had a real HNSW index on the embedding column (`vector_cosine_ops`), but the recall step never used it: it fetched every eligible candidate's full embedding by user id and computed cosine similarity by hand in JavaScript. New SECURITY DEFINER RPC `match_candidates_by_embedding(p_ids uuid[], p_embedding vector(768), p_model text, p_limit int)` does the same ranking with pgvector's `<=>` operator inside Postgres, letting the query planner use the index (or, when the id list is already small after the must-have prefilter, do exact math over just that set, which is faster and exact rather than approximate — either way beats pulling every candidate's full vector over the wire). Locked to `service_role` only, no `authenticated`/`anon` grant, same restriction shape as `assessment_rubrics`/`assessment_results` (v3.13.0): it returns real candidate profile data for an arbitrary id list with no authorization check of its own, so it must never be callable directly via PostgREST by a signed-in user, only from inside the edge function after org membership and billing are already checked. Verified twice: the SQL function's own ranking against three controlled test vectors (padded 768-dim, one dimension set per candidate) returned exact cosine similarities in the right order; then the full edge function path end to end with a real employer session and three seeded candidates, confirming the Supabase JS client's `.rpc()` call coerces a plain `number[]` (the existing `embedText()` output, no format change needed) to the `vector` parameter type correctly through PostgREST.

Details in docs/map/extension.md and docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.36.0, six real bugs found in a live walkthrough of the production app, fixed and re-verified live, plus the credit bypass v3.35.0 had flagged and left open.

EMPLOYER SIGNUP SILENTLY BECAME A JOB SEEKER. AuthModal.tsx stamped profiles.role and inserted employer_accounts client side, right after signUp() returned; with email confirmation on (it is, in production) there is no session at that instant, so the write ran unauthenticated, RLS filtered it to zero rows, and the surrounding try/catch swallowed the failure with a console.warn. Neither auth.users trigger set role either, so every employer signup silently became a job_seeker with no employer_accounts row, invisible to the admin approval queue. Same shape of bug v3.33.0 already fixed once for consent recording, fixed the same way: handle_new_user_profile now reads role and company_name out of signup metadata inside the same transaction as the account, and self_delete_account's sibling erase_account_core learned to cascade-delete an orgs row on erasure only when the erased user was its last member, so a solo employer's company row does not get orphaned. Verified live with real throwaway accounts on both the employer and seeker paths, and with a two-member org where erasing one member correctly left the org and the other member's row intact.

ERASE_ACCOUNT_CORE CRASHED ON ANY ACCOUNT WITH AI USAGE HISTORY. It nulled llm_usage_logs.user_id, but that column is NOT NULL; the whole erasure rolled back inside its own implicit transaction, so the Delete Account button in Settings and the admin erase path both failed, silently to the person clicking it, for essentially every employer and every extension user. Now deletes those rows instead of nulling them, matching how every other content table in the same function is handled. Verified live by reproducing the exact crash (a real llm_usage_logs row from an employer_spec_extract call), confirming it, then confirming the fix erases cleanly.

RESUME HUB'S JOBS TAB BYPASSED CREDITS. The extension's smart_tailor and ext_cover_letter_text charge COST_TAILOR/COST_COVER; the plain "tailor" and "cover_letter" hub-lane actions JobsTab.tsx calls generated the identical AI output for free, flagged as a metering gap in v3.35.0 and left open then. Both actions now call assertCredits before generating and creditSpend after, same cost constants, same response shape so JobsTab is unaffected beyond a new credits field it does not need to read. Verified live: a real tailor call dropped a real balance from 6 to 4, a real cover letter from 4 to 3, both with matching credit_ledger rows, and an account below COST_TAILOR was refused with insufficient_credits before any AI call ran, not charged then refunded.

COMPANY PROFILE COULD SILENTLY DISCARD UNSAVED FIELDS. CompanyProfile.tsx's sync effect (`useEffect(() => setForm(org), [org])`) overwrote the entire local form on every save, including fields the company size buttons don't wait for a blur to apply. Filling website, industry, headquarters and about, then clicking a company size button before tabbing out of the last field, visibly reset all four to empty and the database confirmed only company_size had saved. The effect now merges: a field only takes the server's incoming value when it still matches the last known server value, so a local edit sitting in an unblurred field survives a save that landed elsewhere. Verified live reproducing the exact repro (fill four fields, click size before blurring) and confirming every field, including the ones with pending edits, persisted correctly.

ADMIN TERMS CONSENT PANE WAS DEAD. get_admin_terms_consent was missing the authenticated GRANT every sibling get_admin_* function has, so it 403'd for every real admin; the function's own has_role check was already correct, just unreachable. One GRANT, matching the existing pattern. Verified live: a temporarily admin-flagged throwaway account got real rows back after the grant, and got refused again the moment the role was removed.

ADMIN PIN ENTRY CAPPED AT 4 DIGITS. admin-auth-pin has always accepted 4 to 6 digit PINs; the login screen rendered exactly 4 boxes and auto-submitted the moment they filled, so a longer PIN had no way to be entered. Now renders 6 boxes; a PIN shorter than 6 submits on Enter or an Unlock button once 4 or more digits are in, a full 6 still auto-submits same as before. Verified by temporarily swapping the stored PIN hash for a known 6-digit test value (never touching or seeing the founder's real PIN), confirming the backend accepts it, then restoring the original hash and attempt counter exactly.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.35.0, metering visibility, real sessions, and a cold load investigation that overturned its own first answer along the way.

CREDIT AND USAGE VISIBILITY. ext_bootstrap now returns credits: { balance, costs }, so the extension sidepanel shows a balance pill on load rather than only after a spend or a failure; the same numbers now show in Resume Hub (a credits pill from the existing billing_get call) and in EmployerHub (searches, proposals and assessments used against the plan, from the existing employer_billing_get call, refreshed after every search, proposal and assessment). All reused existing backend responses; only ext_bootstrap gained a field. Verified live with a throwaway account. Found and flagged rather than fixed: Resume Hub's own Jobs tab tailors and writes cover letters through a separate, older resume-hub action that never calls creditSpend, so the identical output that costs credits from the extension is free from the web app, a real metering gap, not a display gap.

REAL SESSIONS. Active Sessions and Sign Out All in Settings read and wrote device_fingerprints, a table with zero writers anywhere in this codebase, so the list was always empty and Sign Out All silently signed out only the current tab despite its own confirm dialog promising every device. self_list_sessions() and self_revoke_session(p_session_id) now read and write the real auth.sessions, and Sign Out All calls supabase.auth.signOut({ scope: 'global' }), GoTrue's own mechanism, instead of a hand rolled one. Verified live with two concurrent real sessions: listed correctly with the right one flagged current, a single revoke made that session's refresh token fail on its next use, and a global sign out from one session's token invalidated a separate session too. Also driven through the actual Settings UI, not only curl.

THE COLD LOAD, AND A CORRECTED FIRST ANSWER. The first pass diagnosed this from a local dev server: SubscriptionContext (the retired consulting product's billing system, still mounted app wide) was calling three edge functions that looked deleted, one console error per page load. That diagnosis was wrong and was reverted before shipping: those functions (check-subscription, create-checkout, customer-portal) are genuinely deployed, gated by CORS to https://aynn.io only, which is exactly why they failed from localhost and would not fail in production, and the failing call only fires for a signed in session, never on the anonymous "cold visit" the report describes. Measured directly against the live site instead: domContentLoaded ~1.14s, ~540KB total transfer, nowhere near 45 seconds, not reproduced. One small real bug was found and fixed: useFeatureFlags fetched on every new mount instead of checking whether its own module level cache had already loaded, costing a duplicate get_feature_flags round trip on a single landing page load. The structural finding that plausibly explains a report this severe: the project's Postgres lives in eu-west-2 but every edge function response carries x-sb-edge-region: us-east-1, confirmed three times against two different functions; every query inside every edge function call is paying a transatlantic round trip, and a signed in dashboard fires several such calls on mount. This is a platform region setting, not a code change, and was surfaced rather than touched.

EDGE FUNCTION INVENTORY WAS WRONG. list_edge_functions against the live project returned 39 ACTIVE functions, not the eight this file has claimed since v3.27.0. Corrected below, with two items that need the founder's own look rather than an assumption from here: ayn-unified is live, created 2 August 2026, with no matching source anywhere in this repo, and delete-account is live but orphaned, nothing in src/ calls it since v3.34.0 moved deletion to self_delete_account.

Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.34.0, three pieces landed together and none of them had reached these map files until now.

ACCOUNT SELF SERVICE. The Delete Account button in Settings had 404'd since the v3.21.0 cleanup deleted the edge function it posted to, functions/v1/delete-account, leaving a broken legal commitment sitting behind a live button. The removal routine admin_erase_account already used was pulled out into erase_account_core(p_user_id, p_actor, p_reason), and self_delete_account(p_confirm_email) now calls it for the signed in user's own account only, refusing admins and requiring the typed email to match exactly before it runs. self_export_account() returns one jsonb object covering profile, career profile, skills, resumes and tailored versions, cover letters, saved jobs, job matches, discovery consent, proposals, assessments (questions and answers, never the rubric or the score, per v3.13.0), settings, preferences, memory, legal consent history, subscription and credit ledger, downloaded client side as JSON. self_pause_account() is the lighter alternative offered in the same dialog: it turns discoverability off, nulls candidate_index.indexed_at so the person drops out of search immediately rather than at the next reindex, and switches off every email flag in user_settings, without deleting anything. src/lib/account.ts holds all three calls plus the DELETION_REMOVED and DELETION_KEPT copy shown before anyone confirms, and src/components/settings/PrivacySettings.tsx is the new home for export, pause and delete, replacing the dead POST. Verified live on 2 August 2026 against a real throwaway account: export returned real rows, pause flipped talent_pool_consent and user_settings, a wrong confirmation email was refused, and the real delete left auth.users banned a hundred years out with the email scrubbed to erased+...@erased.invalid, profiles/user_settings/terms_consent_log/identities/sessions at zero rows, an account_erasures row written, a high severity audit log entry, and the account unable to sign back in.

BILLING SELF SERVICE. stripe-billing gained resume (undo a cancellation before the period ends, the action existed but nothing called it), change_plan (move up, move down, or down to Free, proration on the way up and a stop at period end on the way down, matching the founder decision that downgrades behave like cancellation: no proration, no refund), state (the subscription's current status so the screen can tell renewing from ending from none) and invoices (receipt history straight from Stripe, up to 24, with a link to each PDF). src/pages/Billing.tsx now shows all of it: a full tier grid including the employer tiers Growth and Scale that the public pricing page deliberately omits, a Keep my subscription button when a cancellation is pending, a billing history list, and a fixed note that unused credits expire at period end and do not roll over.

BRANDING CONSISTENCY. Three different company names were live at once: "AYN Intelligence" in the landing footer, "Advanced AI Solutions" in the shared footer, "AYN AI" (the registered name) in the legal documents. src/components/shared/siteLinks.ts is now the one source for COMPANY_NAME ("AYN AI"), COPYRIGHT_LINE and LEGAL_LINKS, read by both the landing page footer (src/components/landing/LandingSections.tsx) and the shared Footer.tsx, which also closed the gap where Security, Subprocessors and Copyright were missing from the footer entirely. Left behind and unused: footer.copyright in src/i18n/en.ts, ar.ts and fr.ts still says "AYN Intelligence", but nothing reads that key any more since both footers render COPYRIGHT_LINE directly; safe to delete, not yet done.

Not covered by v3.34.0, still open from the same punch list: no credit or usage numbers are shown in the extension or the employer hub despite resume-hub already returning credits and costs on every metered action; there is no active-session list with sign out everywhere; there is no minimum supported extension version check; and the reported 45 second cold load has not been investigated. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.33.0 consent recording is real again. The write had been dead since 7 May 2026: the only writer was useAuth.acceptTerms behind the TermsModal in the legacy dashboard shell, unreachable once seekers were routed to /resume-hub and that shell was deleted, and the v3.30.0 replacement, a browser call after signUp, never fired because with email confirmation on there is no session yet. Both dead paths are gone. The version now comes from the markdown itself, src/lib/legal.ts reading src/content/legal/terms.md and privacy.md, so it cannot drift from the document the person was shown. The acceptance is written by handle_new_user inside the same transaction as the account, from the signup metadata AuthModal attaches, and a failed consent insert fails the signup. An account created without an acceptance being presented, OAuth or by hand, still gets a row with null versions and false flags, so a hole is never left and an acceptance is never invented. The IP arrives afterwards through legal_consent_record, which now completes the existing row instead of writing a second one. The 16 accounts with nothing on file were backfilled as 'backfill_unrecorded'. Re-acceptance is storage only: legal_version_num plus get_admin_consent_gap can find everyone below the current version, the flow itself is deliberately not built. The Overview shows accounts_without_consent, which should be zero, and the account detail view states Recorded or Never captured in words. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.32.1 legal documents, final text. The eight markdown files at src/content/legal/<slug>.md now hold the delivered documents rather than placeholders: Terms of Service 1.0, Privacy Policy 1.0, Cookie Policy 1.0, Security Overview 1.0, Subprocessors 1.0, Data Processing Agreement 1.0, Service Level Agreement 1.0 and Copyright Policy 1.1. They are rendered by one component, src/components/legal/LegalPage.tsx, with the registry and the parsing helpers in src/lib/legalDocs.ts. react-markdown with remark-gfm renders tables; version, effective date and last updated date are read from the top of each file and the header shows the effective date when there is one and the updated date otherwise; stripDocHeader removes the title and those lines from the rendered body so a page states its version once; a table of contents appears at ten or more sections; every heading carries an anchor; the measure is 46rem and there is a real print stylesheet. /terms and /privacy keep their routes; /legal, /cookies, /security, /subprocessors, /dpa, /sla and /copyright are new. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.30.0 legal and consent machinery. Four pieces the policy documents promise and the product did not have. One, cookie consent that actually gates analytics: the Google Analytics snippet is out of the static head, src/lib/analytics.ts loads gtag only after an explicit accept, src/components/shared/CookieConsent.tsx offers Accept and Reject with identical weight and one click each, strictly necessary cookies are named as exempt, the choice is stored with a timestamp and a version and can be changed from either footer, and navigator.globalPrivacyControl true is recorded as a reject with no banner shown. Two, a real /do-not-sell page saying plainly that AYN does not sell personal information and does not share it for cross context behavioural advertising, with how a California resident exercises their rights. Three, a consent record: src/lib/legal.ts holds the one copy of the versions (Terms 1.0, Privacy 1.0, effective 1 August 2026) that the signup checkbox and the stored row both read, terms_consent_log gained privacy_version, ip_address and source, the IP read server side by the new resume-hub action legal_consent_record, and get_admin_account_detail returns legal_consent plus up to ten earlier acceptances for the account detail view. The table is append only so a later material change is a new row, but the reprompt flow is deliberately not built. Four, cancellation terms at the point of purchase: the Stripe Checkout session carried nothing before this release, and now custom_text.submit.message states that the plan renews automatically, can be cancelled at any time, that cancellation takes effect at the end of the period already paid for and that fees are not refunded, backed by real in product cancel and resume actions in stripe-billing and a Cancel subscription button in Billing. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.29.0 per account limits and erasure. Two things sit on top of v3.28.0. One, an override for a single account: account_limit_overrides carries proposals_limit, assessments_limit, searches_limit and monthly_credits, each nullable, with the reason and the admin who set it. Null falls back to the plan, which stays the default for everyone, and 0 is a real zero, decided in one place by effectiveLimit in resume-hub so planLimitReached and the 402 message stay honest about who set the number, with the same override read by the stripe-webhook credit grant. An override is per user, so it survives a plan change and shows as a badge in the account detail view next to plan, override and what is in force. Two, real removal in two levels: admin_erase_account deletes the person's content across roughly fifty tables and their storage objects, keeps credit_ledger and subscriptions for accounting and keeps the proposals and assessments they received with the candidate reduced to an opaque erased-xxxxxxxx ref, then bans the auth user and strips its email, phone and metadata; admin_purge_account removes the auth.users row itself and only on an already erased account. Both require admin, refuse you and other admins, require a reason, and require the typed email to match exactly, and both audit at high severity in a place that survives the deletion. Two side effects were needed: the dead on_security_event trigger was dropped, and reveal_requests.candidate_user_id is now nullable with ON DELETE SET NULL so a purge cannot delete the employer's own history. Verified live with two throwaway accounts. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.28.0 account moderation. The admin can finally see how a person signed up and act on the account. The Accounts pane opens each row into a read only detail view (auth provider, email confirmation, signup and sign in history, resume, profile completeness, discoverability, saved jobs, proposals both ways, assessments, credits balance and spend, role, employer status, plan) and offers two levers beside it. Suspension is all or nothing: admin_suspend_account sets auth.users.banned_until with a mandatory reason, admin_restore_account clears it, both audit at high severity, and neither will touch your own account or another admin. Restrictions are per capability: discovery, proposals, assessments and ai, stored in account_restrictions with the reason and who set it. Everything is enforced server side by accountGate in resume-hub, on the web lane and the extension lane, in the order global kill switch, suspension, capability, answering 403 with account_suspended or account_restricted in the same shape as the v3.25.0 maintenance answers. Verified live against a real non admin account. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.27.0 admin panel audit, the last four fixes. One, the Settings pane no longer lies. It used to write ten system_config keys that nothing outside the pane ever read, including a second maintenance mode. There is now exactly one maintenance mechanism, the v3.25.0 platform kill switch, and the pane keeps only the admin PIN plus a pointer to Kill switches; the ten dead keys were deleted from system_config and useAdminSystemConfig / useSetSystemConfig are gone. Employer approval stays unconditional, credits come from plans, and login attempts and session length belong to Supabase auth. Four, searches are a real plan allowance (plans.searches_limit, 25 on the free month, 100 Starter, 400 Growth, 1200 Scale) enforced by planLimitReached, with the 200 soft cap kept only for a plan that records no limit, and a declined employer gets employer_status 'declined' instead of 'suspended', which is now reserved for an employer who was approved and then stopped. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.26.0 admin panel audit, four fixes. One, candidate_index.indexed_at and candidate_index.embedding_model are nullable now, so the Candidates reindex button (admin_mark_candidates_stale nulls both) no longer throws a not null violation on the first real candidate; employer_match already coerces with (r.embedding_model || FALLBACK_EMBED_MODEL), so a nulled row is treated as stale and rebuilt by the bounded 25 per run inline reindex, and the update was tested against a live row. Two, the 'duty' role is dropped from the admin gate: every admin RPC checks has_role(auth.uid(),'admin') only, so duty could pass the PIN and then read "Admin access required" everywhere, and it is now denied at the door. Three, the PIN can no longer be skipped from devtools: AdminApp checks user_roles before it honours any cached flag, and the cache holds an HMAC signed 8 hour ticket minted by admin-auth-pin, re-verified through a new check { ticket } action, instead of a forgeable user id. Four, the settings write in AdminPanel moved onto adminRpc like every other admin call.

Preceded by v3.25.0 the kill switches are real. The admin Kill switches pane used to write flags nothing read. Now resume-hub reads get_feature_flags through the service client with a 30 second cache and refuses the work behind each switch with a 503 carrying { code: "feature_disabled", feature, message }, handle_new_user raises when signups is off, and the frontend shows the switch message in place of the feature instead of an error toast. Six keys: platform, candidate_search, proposals, assessments, tailoring, signups, all defaulting to on. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

Preceded by v3.13.0 verification assessments. An employer can send a candidate a short assessment before deciding whether to spend a proposal. Questions are generated from that candidate's own claimed background against the JobSpec of the search they were found in, and the prompt (QUALITY_MODEL) probes lived experience rather than textbook knowledge, so doing the work is the only way to answer well. Three new tables, and the split between them is the security design: assessments (candidate readable, SELECT only, anon has nothing, all writes via service_role), assessment_rubrics and assessment_results (ALL privileges revoked from anon and authenticated, service_role only, verified with has_table_privilege after the migration). The candidate never sees a score, only growth notes; the employer sees score, verdict, per question observations and the milliseconds spent per question, because a flawless four paragraph answer written in eleven seconds is the signal. The timer is enforced from the server side started_at, never a client clock. Ten new resume-hub actions: employer_assessment_generate, employer_assessment_send, employer_assessment_list, assessment_list, assessment_start, assessment_answer, assessment_submit, assessment_growth_notes. UI: AssessmentDialog and AssessmentsPanel on a new employer Assessments tab, "Send an assessment" beside "Send a job proposal" in the candidate dialog, and a badged Assessments tab in Resume Hub for the candidate. July 2026.

Preceded by v3.12.0 employer surface, properly. Nine fixes from real testing. The buttons were still black because the shadcn Button variants in this repo are written with bg-foreground and border-foreground, not bg-primary, so redefining --primary under .employer-surface never reached them; index.css now retints button.bg-foreground and button.border-foreground inside the scope (measured: primary resolves to rgb(249,112,21), outline stays transparent, both 44px tall, with a real gap and Start over clearly secondary). The proposal draft prompt was rewritten from a match report into an invitation: greeting, one line on the company, at most two specifics about the person, an invitation to talk, then what happens next, with skills-with-years and the phrase "must-have skills" explicitly forbidden. Intake persistence was broken two ways: the save effect returned early while the phase was "opening" so the free text opener was never written at all, and the step position was never stored, so a restored draft reopened at the first unanswered question; the step now travels inside phase as "asking:<step>" (column read widened from 24 to 64 chars). The candidate background block no longer renders profile_text: employer_match attaches a structured buildCandidateProfile block (header, skills grouped by level, experience rows, education, seeking) that never emits a label for an empty value, and buildProfileText itself was fixed to stop producing "YoE: ." and "Education: BSc  at". EmployerHub now has an AYN branded header with a company menu (Company profile, Sign out), a left nav in the Resume Hub language (Search, Proposals, Company) that is not rendered while the v3.11.0 gate is closed, and the company profile behind a dialog rather than a card in the flow. Resume Hub's "Back" button was replaced by the AYN mark with Sign out in a right side menu. The legacy dashboard is deleted (src/components/Dashboard.tsx and src/components/dashboard/DashboardContainer.tsx) and /dashboard plus /dashboard/* redirect to "/", which routes by role. July 2026.

Preceded by v3.11.0 company profile first. The company profile is a required onboarding step, not a settings form: an employer cannot run a candidate search or send a proposal until name, website, industry, headquarters, company size and an about paragraph of at least 80 characters are filled in (LinkedIn and logo stay optional). While required fields are missing, EmployerHub renders ONLY the onboarding company profile, and the same rule is enforced server side by assertOrgProfileComplete in resume-hub, which returns 428 from employer_spec_extract, employer_match and employer_reveal_request. Clearing a required field later re-locks the surface in place and names the field. Nudges are specific lines per missing field, no percentage bar. July 2026.

Preceded by v3.6.0 the proposal loop. The two sides of AYN are connected end to end for the first time. An approved employer no longer lands in the seeker dashboard: Index.tsx AuthedShell routes them to src/pages/EmployerHub.tsx, which finally calls employerApi.intake, .match and .sendProposal (all three had zero callers before this release). The employer describes a role, edits the JobSpec, reads up to three candidates with score, evidence, gaps and a skills provenance split, and sends a JOB PROPOSAL: job title, location, employment type, salary range, link to the posting, and a message up to 1000 characters. reveal_requests is now the proposals table (same name, eight new columns) with two rate limits: one open proposal per org and candidate at a time, and no new proposal within 30 days of a decline. The seeker reads proposals on a new Proposals tab in Resume Hub, badged with the pending count and surfaced as the first Home next-action; accepting is the only thing that releases name, email and phone. Preceded by "v3.0.1 tracker removal" and "v3.0.0 autofill removal — the extension is read only. Deleted the entire write path and the fill-only backend surface. Permissions are activeTab, storage, sidePanel, webNavigation with https only."

## What AYN is

AYN is a job search product with two sides.

For job seekers: a Chrome extension that reads the job posting off the page, scores it against their resume and profile, and generates a tailored resume and cover letter for that specific role. Plus Resume Hub, a web app holding one resume, one profile, saved jobs, and proposals.

For employers: a chat that turns a described role into a structured spec, searches candidates who opted into discovery, returns the three best fits with the evidence behind each, and lets them send an assessment or a job proposal. Contact details are shared only when the candidate accepts.

Stack: React and Vite frontend, Supabase (Postgres, pgvector, edge functions, auth, storage), Stripe for billing, Chrome MV3 extension.

Matching: a deterministic prefilter on extracted skills, then vector recall, then a grounded rerank. Candidates are never invented and skills are tagged extracted or inferred.

One repo, one Supabase backend (project dfkoxuokfkttjhfjcecx). Solo founder: Ghazi. Site ayn.careers (moved from aynn.io in v3.85.0). Deployed via Lovable (project a2fa8496-aed3-4f21-93fc-bbbabc069583) which pushes to this GitHub repo.

| Area | What it is | Map file |
|---|---|---|
| **Connectivity blueprint** | **Read before touching ANY feature, new or existing.** A change in this app is never one file — a new paid action touches billing, RLS, the resume-hub dispatcher, admin visibility, account erasure, and CORS, minimum, and a new page touches branding scope, desktop and mobile nav, and how anyone actually reaches it. This file is the concrete "when you touch X, also touch Y" checklist for both backend and UI/UX, a system diagram, and the security/scale/AI-feature techniques that actually found this app's real bugs. | docs/map/blueprint.md |
| Chrome extension | Sideloaded MV3 extension, READ ONLY since v3.0.0: reads the real job description off the page, scores the match, tailors resumes and cover letters, answers questions about the job. It never writes to a page. Code: extension/. | docs/map/extension.md |
| Resume Hub | Web workspace at /resume-hub: one profile holding the one active resume, saved jobs with their tailored documents, get discovered, proposals, assessments, extension management. Code: src/components/resume-hub/, src/lib/resumeHub.ts, src/lib/resumeDocs.ts, src/lib/extension.ts. Backend: supabase/functions/resume-hub. | docs/map/resume-hub.md |
| Employer surface | /`EmployerHub`: company profile gate, widget intake to JobSpec, candidate match, ask cards, assessments, proposals. Backend: employer actions in supabase/functions/resume-hub. | docs/map/platform.md |
| Platform | Admin panel, billing and credits, support, landing page, World Intelligence simulator. Code: src/components/admin, support, landing; src/admin-app; src/pages/*. | docs/map/platform.md |

## Edge functions — CORRECTION, this section was wrong (v3.27.0 said eight, there are 39)

The repo's supabase/functions/ holds nine entries (resume-hub, resume-match, stripe-billing, stripe-webhook, sign-document, ayn-agent-society, admin-auth-pin, admin-broadcast, _shared), and the v3.27.0 entry above claimed that was the whole list, "everything else was deleted in v3.21.0." Checked directly against the live project on 2 August 2026 with list_edge_functions: there are 39 deployed functions, ACTIVE, not 9. The extra 30 are live infrastructure this map has never described: file-upload, send-admin-email, custom-auth-emails, security-monitor, cost-monitor, send-notifications, generate-suggestions, send-contact-email, send-reply-email, support-bot, send-ticket-notification, send-ticket-reply, verify-admin-pin, set-admin-pin, admin-notifications, track-visit, approve-pin-change, save-generated-image, check-subscription, create-checkout, customer-portal, delete-account, health, send-email, auth-send-email, api, report-vitals, resend-inbound-webhook, keep-warm, check-email-exists, admin-pin-alert. Two things worth flagging rather than assuming: delete-account is still ACTIVE even though v3.34.0 replaced its only caller (PrivacySettings.tsx) with self_delete_account and nothing in src/ posts to it any more, so it is orphaned, not wired to anything current, safe to leave or retire. ayn-unified is also ACTIVE, created 2 August 2026, version 3, with no matching folder anywhere in this repo's supabase/functions/ (the v3.8.0 entry above says ayn-unified "the whole" function was deleted, and that deletion is real for the repo, but a function with this name exists live on the server with source this repo has never held). That one has not been opened or touched here; it needs the founder's own look before anyone assumes what it is. check-subscription, create-checkout and customer-portal (the retired consulting-product billing system SubscriptionContext.tsx still calls) are also genuinely live and do respond, just gated by CORS to https://aynn.io only, which is why they fail with FunctionsFetchError from a local dev origin and silently succeed or silently fail in production depending on real Stripe product state, not because they were deleted. Full audit of what the other ~20 do, and whether any belong in this map permanently, is not done. Details in docs/map/platform.md.

See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup, including the stale-secret finding (that address is still `noreply@aynn.io`, the retired domain, pending the founder confirming the real one).

UPDATE v3.134.0: `job-board-sync` added — a new, real edge function, cron-scheduled every 4 hours, pulling real job postings from company career pages (via freehire's public API, never LinkedIn or Indeed) into the new `job_postings` table, filtered against a real aggregator-domain denylist and a non-Latin-script check, pruned past a 7-day freshness window every run. See the top-of-file entry for the full writeup, including the two real, live-caught data-quality bugs (aggregator links hiding in freehire's own results; a truncated-description endpoint) fixed before this was trusted.


## Global rules (apply everywhere)

1. GENERATED, never hand-edit: public/ayn-extension.zip and public/ayn-extension-version.json. Run node extension/build.mjs.
2. Version bump protocol: manifest.json version + content.js AYN_BUILD fallback, then build.mjs (which rewrites the version file).
3. Never write to the applications or job_applications tables. Both trackers are deprecated and the Tracker UI was deleted in v3.0.1.
4. The extension is read only. Never add code that writes to, clicks, or types into a page.
5. User-facing writing style: no em dashes, no en dashes, ranges use "to". This rule is also baked into the AI system prompts in cc-generate and resume-match.
6. servers: server.js is the express static host for dist/ (SPA fallback, caching for /assets and /frames). backend/server.py is a FastAPI health stub only, not a real backend. The real backend is Supabase edge functions.
7. memory/PRD.md describes an older platform-era snapshot; trust the docs/map files over it.
8. scripts/check-wiring.mjs must pass; it verifies sidepanel messages have handlers, extension actions are registered, and Hub actions are session-reachable.


## The nervous system (how signals actually flow)

```
                    ayn.careers (Resume Hub)
                    │  AYN_PROFILE_UPDATED / AYN_PING / handoff deep link
                    ▼
 job page ───► extension sidepanel + background.js ───► resume-hub edge fn ───► Supabase tables
 (content.js         JD resolver ladder                 (14 ext actions,          (jobs, resumes,
  read only)         score / tailor / cover / ask        2 auth lanes)             ai_result_cache)
                    ▲
                    └── saved jobs, scores and applications ──► Hub tabs read the same tables
```

Four loops carry everything:

1. READ LOOP: content.js extracts the JD from the live page (site selector map, JSON-LD and meta fallback); the background JD resolver ladder (manual paste, current page, opener tab, registry, listing fetch, backend lookup) upgrades it until jdQuality >= 45. Everything downstream is grounded on that text.
2. SYNC LOOP: profile edited in the Hub -> AYN_PROFILE_UPDATED clears the extension's cached identity -> next read refetches. 24h TTL is the fallback for closed browsers.
3. GAP LOOP (v3.1.0): _shared/tailoring.ts computes matched / missing / nice-to-have deterministically from the JD against structured sections, the model only surfaces and phrases, and the same analysis is returned to the sidepanel so the user sees what is genuinely missing from their background.
4. HANDOFF LOOP: Hub tailors a resume for a job -> deep link carries resumeId -> sidepanel preselects that resume_versions row for scoring, tailoring, and cover letters.
5. MATCH LOOP (v2.9.0-B): seeker opts in -> indexCandidate builds anonymized profile_text + 768d embedding + extracted/inferred skills -> employer_intake_chat distills a JobSpec -> employer_match runs extracted-only prefilter (must-haves), pgvector recall (top 12), then a single grounded rerank on opaque refs (inferred capped at 10 pts) -> top 3 anonymous cards. The ref_map that binds refs to real users never leaves the edge function.
6. PROPOSAL LOOP (v3.6.0): employer opens a card in EmployerHub and reads the full anonymous reasoning -> sends a job proposal (title, location, type, salary, link, message ≤1000 chars) through employer_reveal_request, rate limited to one open proposal per org and candidate and blocked for 30 days after a decline -> the row lands in reveal_requests -> the seeker sees it on the Resume Hub Proposals tab, badged in the rail and first in Home next-actions -> reveal_decide records accept or decline -> ONLY on accept does employer_reveal_status return name, email and phone. Everything before that step is anonymous, enforced in the edge function, not in the UI.


## Honest assessment (strengths, weaknesses, what is actually smart)

STRENGTHS AND MOAT
- Everything AYN now promises runs on our own backend: JD extraction, match scoring, tailoring, cover letters, talent pool matching. No hostile territory, no probability disguised as a guarantee.
- JD grounding is the real asset: a six-tier resolver plus a quality score means the AI is never asked to judge a job from a nav bar and a cookie banner.
- Unified identity (_shared/identity.ts) feeds scoring, tailoring, and cover letters from one place, so quality fixes land everywhere at once.
- Device-token auth (scoped, revocable, no passwords).

WEAKNESSES AND RISKS
- supabase/functions/resume-hub/index.ts is still a large monolith with no unit tests (e2e/ covers the legacy platform, not the extension). content.js is down to ~970 lines after v3.0.0.
- Distribution: sideload only. No auto update, no Chrome Web Store trust signal; users silently rot on old builds. Biggest product risk today.
- The legacy platform roughly doubles repo weight, confuses tooling and new contributors, and its e2e suites can mislead CI signals.
- Bus factor of one (solo founder). These map files are the mitigation; keeping them current is not optional.

WHAT IS ACTUALLY SMART HERE (for an AI forming a mental model fast)
The core insight that survived v3.0.0: on modern job pages, the DOM is a liar and the visible text is the truth. That is why the reader (site selectors, JSON-LD fallback, quality scoring, the resolver ladder) is the part worth defending. Writing to those same pages was the part that could never be made reliable, so it is gone. Judge any proposed change against that split: read deeply, never write.
